ParlaMint-NO_2013-06-20.ana.ud2501e3809	Developments in this matter since the start of the examination of Prop. 119 S in the middle of April, says more than words about the significant values in the company Cermaq ASA. The struggle for market shares for farmed salmon and fish feed in the world market is tough and intense. It also carries the battle of Cermaq clearly off. Cermaq ASA is a company that the government of the ownership notice has placed in category 1 of government-owned companies. In this way, the government is acting in full line with the business judgements made in relation to state ownership in this category. The ownership message opens both for strategic purchase and full sale of the country's category 1 shareholdings. For us in the Christian People's Party, however, this case has more aspects of it than purely commercial ones. We record that the State can collect a fine gain on its investments in Cermaq ASA by a proper course of action when it sells in full. We also see that a purchase up to 65 pst. participation may be necessary to control the game of the company. Nevertheless, as we evaluated it as late as April this year, there are other considerations that weigh more heavily in assessing the appropriateness of a full sale in Cermaq ASA. In the ownership notification, the government provides some overall reasons for state ownership as a principle in the Norwegian mixed economy. One of these reasons is particularly important for the Christian People's Party: there is the possibility of raising expectations about social responsibility to companies in which the state has interests. Such expectations of social responsibility will not be equally relevant to all companies with State ownership or to all transactions in which the State changes its ownership portfolio. Cermaq ASA is a company that has located the majority of its business activities outside its borders and the majority of its employees are not resident in Norway. Through the EMU business area, Cermaq has a global market share of 36 pst. in fish feed, and through the Mainstream business area a global market share of approximately 6 pst. within the rearing of salmon and trout. This means that the management of Cermaq ASA is related to daily important ethical and practical considerations as regards the treatment of workers ashore with other labour law conditions, the management of scarce fish resources such as raw material for fish feed and significant environmental challenges related to fish farming. We therefore consider that this is a matter in which the state's ability to raise expectations of social responsibility is particularly important from the company's business area. On page 56 of the Communication of Ownership, government expectations on social responsibility include: specified in the following points: ‘The Government expects that: Companies with international activities are linked to the United Nations Global Compact and that companies with international supply chains consider the same. [[...]] [[...]] International companies or international supply chains are at the heart of the eight core conventions of the ILO.’» The continued state ownership of Cermaq ASA will thus allow for the accumulation and government to make clear expectations of the company on social responsibility in these areas in the future. In the event of a full sale of the country's holding, that possibility will lapse. During the current period, the Christian People's Party has repeatedly highlighted the major environmental challenges associated with Norwegian farming. The Christian People's Party has also called for government investment in different companies operating in the international market to be based more strongly on ethical assessments of the company's business operations. In this context, we have noticed that Cermaq ASA places great emphasis on the full and sound reporting of employment for its own employees, the sustainability and enforcement of national and international laws and regulations. It believes that the Christian People's Party is very positive, and in line with the expectations of social responsibility placed by the state as a partner in the company. We believe that the continued state ownership of one of the world's largest fish farms and the production of fish feed in itself can be justified by the possibility of raising expectations of the company on social responsibility and ethically sound business. We would also like to remind you that the Stortinget has voted against the full sale of the state's shares in Cermaq ASA on two occasions during the current growing period. This happened for the first time when the Storting dealt with the ownership notification on 9 June 2011 and again during the treatment of the Storting on a proposed share issue in the company on 14 May of this year. It says that the majority of Stortinget is skeptical about allowing the free games of market forces to determine who will dominate and possibly control the farming industry. This applies both here to the country and to the international. The Christian People's Party will give its support to I and II in the vote. But we're going to vote against III, with the reasons I've given here.

The government believes it is right and important to secure the state's operating space in a situation where Cermaq is in play. I therefore ask that the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Trade should be given the necessary powers, as requested in the submitted proposal, and I am pleased that the majority has agreed to this.
